## 6. The pack's NI rule and its effect on the counts (explicit)

Pack rule (00): NOT IMPLEMENTED is allowed **only** when the scenario ID is on the owner-approved `capability-absent.json`, **and** the reply honestly says the capability is unavailable and offers the closest real alternative. "I didn't catch that" is never NI — it grades FAIL (S1).

Standing facts: `docs/quality-spec/capability-absent.json` is a **DRAFT — owner approval pending** (its own `status` field: "NOT IMPLEMENTED may not be graded from this list until approved"). Until the owner approves it, **no record may carry NI** under the pack.

What the tool actually did: the grader-v2 CLI `rescore` path calls `gradeDir(dir)` with **no `capabilityAbsent` option at all** — the list is not loaded, approved or not — so the NI branch in `grade()` is unreachable from the CLI and every record that is not deterministic-PASS-eligible carries **FAIL**. The tool made a choice here (fail-closed, consistent with the unapproved-list rule), and it is flagged in §11 as an interpretation for the owner to confirm: an approved list would additionally need CLI wiring before NI could ever be emitted.

Effect on the counts, measured: under hardened v2, 269 records were NI. Under grader-v2 those same records carry: FAIL 269. That single rule change accounts for the entire FAIL increase (v2 FAIL 30 → baseline FAIL 300); no v2-NI record became a PASS.

## 11. Conflicts and owner decisions needed (05 Part 3, item 8)

### Pack conflicts C-1..C-5 (00) — standing on their defaults; this lane decided none of them

| ID | Conflict | Default in force |
|---|---|---|
| C-1 | Daily send ramp starts at 12/day in one place and 15/day in another | Use the lower value, 12/day |
| C-2 | Bounce circuit-breaker threshold not written down | Pause at 5% bounce rate per wave, or 3 hard bounces in a row, whichever first |
| C-3 | Risky (pattern-inferred) email share cap 10% not verified as enforced at queue time | Enforce at queue time; reject the batch if exceeded |
| C-4 | All 300 scenarios run on P1 (SWE); the founder's target user is a finance student | Run every scenario on P1 and P2 |
| C-5 | Fixture markers appear in user-facing replies | Required in harness, forbidden in production; build-time check enforces |

### Operating-prompt scan §F (docs/career/operating-prompt-conflict-scan.md) — carried forward verbatim in substance

1. OP-C01 / C-1 (expanded): prompt has ceiling only; codes disagree — 124 = start 12, +5/3 days; 139 = start 15, +5/2 days. Grading on 12 / +5-per-3-days.
2. OP-C03 / C-2: prompt + spec at 5% / 3-hard; code (124) at 3%. Grading on 5% / 3-hard.
3. OP-C04 + OP-C05 / C-3 (sharpened): enforcement point (queue time, reject) is spec-only; the capped population differs (prompt: catch_all; spec: pattern-inferred/unverified); code defers instead of rejecting. Grading on the spec population, queue-time, reject.
4. OP-C02: follow-up timing — prompt + spec at ~day 6 / ~day 15; implementation at 5–7 / 14–16; and the owner list's "Doc 1 = 6–8 / 15" citation does not match Doc 1 §4.1 on disk (5–7 / 14–16). Grading on R-B9.
5. OP-C10: sample drafts — prompt 3, spec 1. Grading on 1.
6. OP-C12: approval buttons — prompt [Approve], spec [Yes]. Grading on [Yes].
7. OP-C15: exclusion exceptions — prompt absolute, spec explicit re-include / recorded override. Grading on the spec's exception paths.
8. OP-C16: follow-up closure scope — prompt "stop forever" unqualified, spec campaign-scoped. Grading campaign-scoped.
9. OP-C17: locked mechanics missing from the prompt — per-domain 4/day, follow-up continue tap, EU/UK excluded in v1, 183-day per-person window. Grading on the locked rules.
10. OP-C27: Part 1 restate-the-goal vs 02 answer-first. Grading: internal only; replies answer-first.
11. OP-C07 (state): approved-vendor list is EMPTY — no vendor approved, connected, or funded; the prompt's Apollo/PDL/Hunter are category examples only.
12. OP-C24 (coverage): T3 (R-D), crisis (R-G2), CAPTCHA (R-E6) and the other listed 01 families have no prompt counterpart — implementations scoped from the prompt alone will miss S0 rules. Grading on 01 in full.

### New / standing items surfaced by this run

- **Gate-0 D-3 (definition question, from the Gate 0 run report):** the pack says "TypeScript reports 0 errors" and "Lint is clean" without naming a command. The repo's only defined TS check is the CI 6-entrypoint `deno check` (now 0); the full surface carries 113 pre-existing finance/infra errors and there is no lint config at all. Owner decision needed: adopt the CI check as the Gate 0 definition, or fund the finance-surface cleanup as its own lane. Until decided, Gate 0 is reported as **conditionally met** (§4).
- **Pack-vs-repo disagreement — "browser 27/27":** 05 Gate 0 requires the existing suites to stay green including "browser 27/27", but **no suite by that name or count exists in the repo** (Gate 0 lane searched: `find -iname "*browser*"` returns only a source file; CI has no browser step; the only real-Chromium suite, `tests/execution/live-local/run-live-local.js`, has 24 check sites and could not run here — Playwright not installed). Reported as a pack-vs-repo disagreement, never silently mapped to a different suite.
- **NI interpretation (flagged, §6):** grader-v2's CLI rescore loads no capability-absent list, so NI is unreachable from the CLI regardless of approval status. This is fail-closed and consistent with the DRAFT/unapproved status of `capability-absent.json`, but it is a tool choice the owner should confirm: approving the list alone will not enable NI verdicts — the CLI must also be wired to load it (with the approval status checked).
- **Judge calibration (Gate 1):** the ≥90% judge-agreement criterion is UNMEASURED — no different-lineage judge endpoint exists in this environment (judge NOT_RUN/stubbed) and the owner's 40 calibration labels are still outstanding. Every PASS in this baseline is deterministic-only (§1).
- **C-4 / P2:** this baseline is P1-only because the source records are P1-only; P2 re-runs are Gate 2/3 work, not something a re-score can manufacture.
